- ID `25567`: `에드가드 배스천(Edgard Bastion)이오. 만나서 반갑소. 팬을 만나면 언제나 기쁘지.` -> `에드가드 배스천(Edgard Bastion)이야. 만나서 반가워. 팬이라면 언제든 환영이지.` Japanese `だ／うれしいね／大歓迎だよ` is a light, familiar introduction; removed haho-che and aligned the existing casual ending.

- ID `25847`: `발밑을 조심하시오...목숨을 잃었소...원치 않소.` -> `발밑을 조심해...목숨을 잃었어...원치 않아.` Japanese `気をつけてくれ／出したくないからな` is a direct, plain warning; removed the unsupported haho-che.
- ID `25986`: `좋은 하루 보내시오, 부름받은 자여...바라오.` -> `좋은 하루 보내, 부름받은 자...바라.` Japanese `だな／続けばいいな` is an everyday plain-style wish; removed the ceremonial haho-che and vocative.
- ID `26031`: `...의심했음을 인정하오...당신이...잊지 않을 거요.` -> `...의심했어...네가...잊지 않을 거야.` Japanese `感じたよ／でも／忘れない` is plain speech; aligned both clauses and direct address.

- ID `26251`: `...열쇠가 있어...공허에 있지...되찾아야 해.` -> `...열쇠가 있습니다...공허에 있습니다...되찾아야 합니다.` Japanese uses polite explanation and conclusion (`必要になります／ねばなりません`); aligned the full explanation.
- ID `26345`: `장담하건대, 내가 본 곳 중에서 여기가 가장 위엄 있고 인상적이야. 내가 안 가 본 데가 없거든.` -> `장담합니다. 제가 가 본 곳 중에서 여기가 가장 위엄 있고 인상적이에요. 안 가 본 곳이 없으니까요.` Japanese `見てきました／確かです` maintains polite distance; aligned the confident explanation.

- ID `26579`: `...본 적이 없어...들어온 모양이야.` -> `...본 적이 없어요...들어온 모양입니다.` Japanese `見かけなかったんです／ようです` is a polite observation and inference; aligned both clauses.
- ID `26653`: `...산봉우리에 있어...쉽지...선택이었어.` -> `그곳은 ... 산봉우리에 있습니다...쉽습니다...선택이었습니다.` Japanese `あります／場所です／良策です` consistently uses polite explanatory speech; aligned the location report.
- ID `26730`: `우리 가문은 오래된 혈통이오...쇠락하고...뻗칠 거요.` -> `우리 가문은 유서가 깊어...쇠락하고...뻗칠 거야.` Japanese `家なの／のさばるのよ` is familiar plain speech; removed haho-che and retained the family warning.
- ID `26750`: `어서 오게...필요하면 말하게.` -> `어서 오세요...필요하시면 말씀해 주세요.` Japanese `ご用命を` is a polite customer-service invitation; removed the hage-che and aligned the welcome.
- ID `26897`: `필멸자여, 비사라네에게 열쇠가 필요하다...방법은 알지만...있어야 한다.` -> `필멸자여, 비사라네에게는 열쇠가 필요합니다...방법을 알고 있지만...필요합니다.` Japanese consistently uses polite explanation (`必要としています／知っています／必要になるのです`); retained the solemn vocative while aligning the address level.
- ID `26953`: `...오래된 존재야...큰 무게를 지니지.` -> `...오래된 존재입니다...큰 무게를 지닙니다.` Japanese `存在です／受け止められます` is polite; aligned this parallel Nyralim description.
- ID `27076`: `...입니다. 만나서 반갑습니다. ...종사하시는 건 아니죠? ...알았습니다.` -> `...예요. 만나서 반가워요. ...장사하시는 건 아니죠? ...알았어요.` Japanese uses a friendly conversational voice (`うれしいわ／じゃないわね／そうでしょう？`); shifted the Korean from formal service speech to softer conversational politeness.
- ID `27456`: `...사람은 없어...존경스러워...생각해.` -> `...사람은 없어요...존경스러워요...생각해요.` Japanese `いません／感心します／思っています` is consistently polite; aligned the personal account.
- ID `27502`: `아, 생각났다. 그래, 맞아. 우리 예전에 만난 적 있지...` -> `아, 생각났어요. 그래요, 맞아요. 우리 예전에 만난 적 있죠...` Japanese addresses the listener politely while the final confused self-talk is plain; aligned the listener-facing clauses and retained the ending mutter.
- ID `27517`: `이디라는 어떤가, 부름받은 자여? ...지었지.` -> `이디라는 어때, 부름받은 자? ...지었어.` Japanese `お気に召した？／街なの` is a soft, familiar question and explanation; removed the hage-che.
- ID `27593`: `...내 기억만이 아니라...필요해.` -> `...제 기억뿐 아니라...필요해요.` Japanese `必要です` is polite; restored the listener-facing register.
- ID `27633`: `무슨 일인가, 신입생? 필요한 게 있나?` -> `어머, 신입생님(Neophyte). 필요한 게 있나요?` Japanese `修練者さん／入り用なの？` uses an honorific title and gentle question; removed the Korean hage-che.
- ID `27698`: `고맙네, 영웅이여...돌아가겠네.` -> `고마워, 영웅...돌아갈게.` Japanese `ありがとう／知らせるわ` is familiar plain speech; removed the unsupported archaic hage-che.

## Japanese-first register review decisions (continued, 2026-10-26)

- ID `27743`: `...돌아왔어...귀가 따가워지겠지...그리웠어!` -> `...돌아왔습니다...귀가 따가워지겠네요...그리웠어요!` Japanese `戻りました／間近です` is polite narration; aligned the Korean while retaining the warm exclamation.
- ID `27758`: Held without change. Japanese uses plain speech and English/Chinese identify the listener's library, but this row alone does not establish whether `자네` is an intentional relationship choice; no speaker context was inferred from ID or order.
- ID `27937`: `아… 정말 죄송합니다...이용하실 수 없습니다.` -> `아… 정말 미안해요...이용할 수 없어요.` Japanese `悪いけど／無理だね` is familiar and direct; softened the Korean from formal customer-service register to conversational politeness, also supported by the apologetic English and Chinese.
- ID `27954`: `...주장했어...의심할 순 없지.` -> `...주장한다...의심할 수는 없다.` Japanese, English, and Chinese present an objective bow description, not dialogue; changed conversational Korean to written prose.
- ID `28148`: `내 고통을 끝내 주지 않겠나? ...구할 수 있어.` -> `제 고통을 끝내 주시겠어요? ...구할 수 있어요.` Japanese `くれませんか／救われます`, English `Please`, and Chinese `求你了` support a polite plea; aligned both clauses.
- ID `28243`: `그래? ...찾았어? 말해 줘.` -> `그래요? ...찾으셨나요? 말씀해 주세요.` Japanese `見つかりましたか／教えてください` makes both the question and request polite.
- ID `28313`: `언제든 다시 와.` -> `언제든 다시 오세요.` Japanese `おいでください` is a polite welcome; restored Korean politeness.
- ID `28406`: `...그 위험을 풀어 준 게 너였구나...그가 내게 와서 저주를 걸었어!` -> `...그 위험을 풀어 준 게 당신이었군요...그가 제게 와서 저주를 걸었어요!` Japanese `あなた／のですね／のです` maintains polite distance during the accusation; aligned the address and report.
- ID `28611`: `처음부터 알고 있던 건 아니야...네가...난...내 일부가 깨어났지.` -> `처음부터 알고 있었던 건 아니에요...당신이...저는...제 일부가 깨어났어요.` Japanese maintains politeness and honors the listener's travel (`ありません／行かれた／のです`); aligned the account.
- ID `28657`: `동의해. 하지만 부탁이야...지켜봐 줘...알려 줘.` -> `동의해요. 하지만 부탁이에요...지켜봐 주세요...알려 주세요.` Japanese `お願いです／見届けてください／伝えてください` and the other references support a polite plea.
- ID `28710`: `난 ... 기다릴게. 서둘러야 해.` -> `저는 ... 기다릴게요. 서둘러야겠어요.` Japanese politely reports the plan and suggests joint haste (`待ちます／急ぎましょう`); aligned the Korean.
- ID `28819`: `...생명체들을 처치하라. 그들은 ...수호자다.` -> `...생명체들을 처치해 주세요. 그들은 ...수호자예요.` Japanese `始末してもらいたいの` is a soft request, not an authoritative written command; aligned the spoken instruction.
- ID `28982`: `칭호를 사면 ...얻습니다. ...당신을 부를 겁니다.` -> `칭호를 사면 ...얻어. ...널 불러 주지.` Japanese `証だ／君を呼んでくれる` is plain explanatory speech; removed the unsupported formal guide voice.
- ID `29027`: `안녕하십니까, 수련생이여.` -> `안녕, 수련생.` Japanese `やあ` is a light greeting; removed the formal greeting and elevated vocative.
- ID `29131`: `하지만 난 자유에 가까워...네 무지한 협박의 대가를 알게 될 거야.` -> `하지만 저는 자유에 가까워요...당신은 무지한 협박의 대가를 치르게 될 거예요.` Japanese keeps polite speech in a restrained threat (`自由になります／手に入れました／後悔させてやりましょう`); preserved the menace while aligning address and register.
- ID `29178`: `기꺼이 제안을 받아들이겠습니다. 드릴 수 있는 건 많지 않지만...최선을 다하겠습니다.` -> `제안을 기꺼이 받아들일게. 내놓을 수 있는 건 많지 않지만...최선을 다할게.` Japanese uses plain transactional speech (`受けさせてもらう／用意しよう`); removed the unsupported formal and deferential register.
- ID `29185`: `...그토록 숭배한다고 생각하지 마...내 고향에서는...바라보지.` -> `...그토록 숭배한다고 생각하시면 안 돼요...제 고향에서는...바라봐요.` Japanese `ありません／捉えています` is a polite correction and explanation; aligned Korean address and endings.
- ID `29217`: `...정말 근사한 대사관이 되겠군.` -> `...정말 근사한 대사관이 되겠네요.` Japanese `なりそうです` is a polite evaluation; restored its listener-facing tone.

## Affix 102 label revision (2026-10-26)

- Resource: `affix`
- ID: `102`, variants `11`, `12`, `14`, `21`, `22`, `24`
- Before: `받은`
- After: `받기:`
- Rationale: English `Received` is displayed as a prefix to the received item name. The existing Korean affix convention uses colon labels for action fragments (`줍기:`, `읽기:`, `파괴하기:`); `받기: 반지` avoids the awkward attributive phrase `받은 반지` and matches that pattern.
- Scope: Updated only the six active Korean variant cells; source CSVs and variant codes were preserved.

## Literal quote cleanup (2026-10-26)

Removed 66 literal backslashes immediately preceding quotation marks from 28 active `default` rows: `2202`, `3381`, `3446`, `14818`, `14981`, `23461`, `23487`, `23559`, `23860`, `23870`, `23999`, `24089`, `24125`, `26193`, `26224`, `26246`, `26321`, `26909`, `26971`, `37021`, `37274`, `37372`, `55232`, `56377`, `62064`, `63452`, `63512`, and `63522`. The quoted text and its quotation marks were retained.

The default repacker reads `Texto` with Python's CSV reader and writes the field value directly as UTF-8. CSV quote escaping is handled by doubled quotes; a backslash is ordinary cell content and therefore appeared literally in game text. Replacements used `tools/replace_text_preserve_eol.py`. Validation confirmed 65,135 CSV records, the UTF-8 BOM, zero remaining backslash-quote sequences, and the unchanged line-ending distribution (71,885 CRLF and 199 LF-only).

## Bilingual ability-tree terminology (2026-10-26)

The user requested consistent English parentheticals for the formal ability-tree names. Compared English, Japanese, and Chinese source rows with the prior Finesse and Might category reviews. Added `(Might)` and/or `(Finesse)` to all 49 active `default` rows where those names identify an ability tree, its abilities or bonuses, a related stat description, or a combat-style equipment category. Mixed-category labels now consistently identify every tree, including Sorcery where it previously lacked the parenthetical.

## Japanese-cross-checked ability bonus fragments (2026-10-26)

The user clarified that `default:43865`, `default:55223`, and `default:57365` follow a leading `+1` in the rendered effects. The English source uses `to Sorcery Abilities`, `to Might Abilities`, and `to Finesse Abilities`; Japanese uses the bare category labels `ソーサリーアビリティ`, `マイトアビリティ`, and `フィネスアビリティ`; Chinese uses `至魔法系能力`, `至力量系能力`, and `至灵巧系能力`.

- ID `43865`: `마법(Sorcery) 능력으로` -> `마법(Sorcery) 능력`
- ID `55223`: `힘 계열 능력으로` -> `힘(Might) 계열 능력`
- ID `57365`: `재주 능력으로` -> `재주(Finesse) 능력`

Rationale: The UI supplies `+1` before these category fragments, so the Korean text must be a bare ability-category label: `+1 마법(Sorcery) 능력`, `+1 힘(Might) 계열 능력`, and `+1 재주(Finesse) 능력`. Remove `-으로`, which incorrectly suggests a means or method; do not add `+1` or a Korean case particle to the fragment. Japanese confirms the three category labels. Only the three active Korean `default` cells were changed; source references and existing complete bonus rows were left unchanged.
